ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e3382	We're coming to the 1st. Question to the Federal Minister for Social Security, Generations and Consumer Protection, by Mr Haidlmayr, Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e3398	Minister! My question is: 96/M “Are you satisfied with the initial valorization of the care allowance of 2 per cent – 0.25 per cent per year calculated for the last eight years?”
ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e3419	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e3434	Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, I should like to draw your attention to the history of the care allowance. The care allowance was introduced in 1993 and covered by Article 15a contracts between the countries and the Federal Government. These Article 15a contracts provide because 30 percent of the care allowance from the countries, for the national care providers, and 70 percent of the care allowance from the federal government, are to be borne that there is a common approach between countries and the federal government. The care allowance was increased once, in 1995, for all groups. In 1996, there was a redistribution within the individual groups of care providers, where the care providers of Stages 1 and 2 were disadvantaged at the expense of Stages 4, 5, 6 and 7 by redistributing the volume from bottom to top. After that, it was not possible under the Social Democratic Government to adjust the care allowance. First this year, 2004, I was able to increase the care allowance by 2% for the first time. If one looks at the entire period since the existence of the care allowance, it is necessary to note that this increase is not sufficient to pay off the price of the care allowance in the meantime, but one must also consider the overall development of the care allowance. The total amount of the care allowance has been increased by about 13 percent since 1995 because of demographic developments there has been a spread of the care allowance and a change in the care allowance levels 3 and 4. In 2000, for the most affected group in the field of care benefits, namely, for the care providers of stages 4, 5, 6 and 7 there was an increase in the allowance in the care allowance by 50 percent, which must also be considered to be an improvement for care providers. The current increase in the care allowance by 2 percent only found the agreement of all the federal states at the social conference in July 2003. This is in line with Article 15a. Since at the time of the consent of the federal states, the 2004 budget was already on track, only the first opportunity of the federal government to increase the care allowance by 2 percent was given with the 2005 and 2006 budgets. My home country Thankfully, as the first one in March of this year, Carinthia decided to increase the care allowance by 2%. The federal states of Vorarlberg, Tirol, Salzburg and Lower Austria also provided for an adjustment of the care allowance by 2 percent in their national budgets. The other three federal states are still smack in the legal field. However, I hope that in the course of 2005 the similarity between all federal states, with a care allowance increased by 2 per cent, will take place, so that a harmonised development between national care funds and federal care funds for care providers takes place.

Mr Federal Minister, I therefore ask you: 99/M “What changes in the civil process system have you proposed as a consumer protection minister so that model processes can be carried out in the future without the highest cost risk?”
ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e4376	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e4391	Ladies and gentlemen! On the part of my department, a textual proposal was prepared which was discussed and coordinated with the Federal Ministry of Justice, the VKI and representatives of science. At its meeting of 6 March, the Committee of Justice held a meeting of the European Parliament and of the Council. In October 2004, a resolution was adopted asking the Federal Minister for Justice to examine legal possibilities for the economic and appropriate handling of mass complaints. At this meeting of the Justice Committee, it was also agreed that the agreed legislative proposal would be sent to the Federal Ministry of Justice for Justice. After carrying out the review procedure, the opinion was discussed by the Federal Ministry of Justice, my Ministry and the CCI representatives of the Bar Association. A joint compromise text was drawn up, which was transmitted to the parliamentary clubs. This proposal for a law provides that, at the request of an authorised association, its proceedings may be interrupted by the Court of First Instance where a dispute is pending in the same court against the same defendant, in which essentially the same issues of action or law are to be resolved. In the event of interruption, the limitation of claims shall be inhibited. The discussions in the clubs showed that no application for initiative should be submitted. – I strongly regret this result, but unfortunately the parliamentary deliberations cannot be influenced by me. However, I still believe that the proposal, which has been agreed between VKI, my ministry, the Ministry of Justice and the solicitorship, should see the light of the world of the Justice Committee, for I am experiencing a huge sum of money especially in the case of model processes that go over three-digit million amounts, that simple correspondence for each of the parallel procedures is devouring a huge sum of money. It was therefore necessary to be able to finance such procedures as a responsible consumer protection minister in the first place and, if necessary, in the second instance, in my opinion, to conclude a risk liability by a German company that took over the process risk liability, but which has the disadvantage that consumers – if the process is won – will receive a 30 percent reduced share of their actual payments, because this 30 percent process risk liability obviously needs to be overcome. It would therefore be easier if we were to proceed to a reasonable and economic procedure in this area. I also consider this procedure to be urgently necessary in the light of many procedures of small tenants against oil mutilations, because it is There are major misunderstandings in this area. My current procedure was adopted by the European Commission at the European Union level 14 days ago, and I am therefore waiting for what, in addition to the Austrian antitrust authority, the European Commission will now also do in this process against the Austrian oil surplus. I think there is a wealth of areas in which financial weakness of financial strengths should simply be forced into the knees by paralleling several costly sample processes and those who can afford to simply “buy” the others in order not to get a final Judicatur. I therefore believe that, in the sense of consumer protection, it would be time for the Justice Committee to provide this matter with a positive solution.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e4711	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e4726	Ladies and gentlemen! I am very interested in the fact that you, as a young person, are asking this question. The Federal Government's draft for the harmonisation of pensions is aimed at achieving a level playing field. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Riepl.]] With a view to ensuring the confidence and security of the younger generation in a high-performance and contribution-oriented pension scheme, the creation of a contribution-oriented personal pension account is in line with the principle of the principle that the amount of pensions of an insured person at the same age depends on the amount of contributions made for him. This draft of the proposed gradual harmonisation of contribution rates and the basis for contributions while at the same time harmonising benefits creates the basis for a uniform pension right for all employees. The young generation, in particular, will benefit from this because, in the event of a delay in the necessary reforms, the young generation would have had to carry the benefits for three generations for years to come. I therefore believe that a first stretch of the long transitional periods and thus a harmonious burden of three generations over years is giving young people the opportunity, first of all, to devote as much of their income as possible to the quality of their living and their own life, and secondly, to get money free by reducing the tax and tax rate in order to have enough capital for the second and third pillars of pension insurance. Thirdly, the redundancies have also created a possibility for micro-incomes to move into a personal pension provision in the second pillar – without having to turn away from their daily income. Moreover, it is clear to the young generation from the introduction of harmonised pension rights that it will still have one of the best pension insurance schemes in Europe, including in relation to the amount of benefits. I mean, ladies and gentlemen, that this pension reform has two real winners: On the one hand, the women who have provided family benefits and care services are the younger generation as a whole. It should not be forgotten, however, that it will be easier for the older generation to have it in the future, because the increase in pensions will no longer be in line with the net pension principle introduced in 1995, but as agreed at the negotiating table with all the social partners, namely according to the life-keeping index of the Austrian Statistical Centre. This valorization will bring a clear advantage for the elderly, because I know very few people in Austria – apart from the professionals – who knew and were satisfied with the old net pension adjustment system. If you draw all these points together, this is the calming for those who are now in retirement that they will be upgraded with the price of their pensions from the year 2007. For the young generation, this means that she can finally trust again to get a high-performance pension for herself. With the reduction of the tax and tax rate, the boys will benefit in their active time by having more money for themselves in the wallet and will have to pay less money for the state. In this year's tax reform, it is currently 18 days that each employee has won. The tax reform will add a few more days next year. And when it comes to this federal government, the reduction of the tax and tax rate from – starting point – 45 percent will at least release 7 percent of income for own needs, self-sufficiency, own consumption and for your own lifestyle by 2010. I therefore believe that the young generation in particular can be very satisfied with this pension harmonisation. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Moreover, we have also noted in this pension harmonisation that every three years all the parameters of the economic predictions and the demographic predictions must be checked, so that both young and old generations can rely on the Austrian system, with its high first component of the generation contract, to continue to be adapted and adapted in the future, and not as a foreign body, no matter how the economy in Austria and the economic environment develop, as monoliths remain up and down and therefore entails additional burdens or additional losses. I also regard the entire system as a slim system in its final development phase. You can convince yourself that the legislative text of the new General Pension Act for the period after 2034 covers eight pages, and in these eight pages the whole new pension system is packed. I think there are very few laws in the Second Republic that have such a comprehensive character and yet regulate the future in such a economical form. However, it should also be noted that the transitional provisions require considerable effort. You can imagine the picture: If you are pursuing a common goal in ten different ways, these paths are usually very engulfed, very different in length and very different in design. And that's the way it is when it comes to the harmonisation of pensions. We are based on state-political laws that must be brought together into a simple and common system. As a result, the transition phase is also difficult. We must bring the different systems together into a common system of constitutional and forward-looking cooperation. Deputies! I hope I have answered your question for the young generation, but also for the other generations.

Therefore: These are not final findings, but only social estimates that one has to take seriously, but which do not have to come exactly that way.

And for the second time, for the first time, three senior representatives in this representation group will be able to take up the third social partners for 1.7 million insured persons, namely also with a seat and voice, and not only as listeners as before. [[Abg. Oillinger: Party organizations!]] We are thus delivering a promise to the senior pensioners' associations which have been made by many political groups in this House to the elderly, and which, in my view, is important in view of the interests represented in the main association of the Austrian social security institutions. Since Mr Gusenbauer has carried out that there is only a "reversive action" here, I must say that this claim can in no way be contradicted here in the room! Mr Gusenbauer, your group has filed a so-called third case on the main association of the Austrian social security companies. In this third case, the Socialist Group of the Austrian Parliament has also disputed the corresponding parity in the composition of the main association in the current form. There is a procedure of the Austrian Constitutional Court, which has concerned itself with a number of paragraphs of its own right and on the provisions of §§ 441b, 441c para. 1st para. 441st para. 1 to 3 and 442a ASVG. The audit therefore also covered the Board of Directors, where there was a parity between the representatives of the service providers and the servants with six members of 14 respectively. The Constitutional Court has not given rise to the parity of service providers and staff members in the appointment of the Board of Directors in the present form, but instead the provision has been withdrawn solely because it has started the occupation procedure and has given rise to the grounds for exclusion after the challenge by Mr Haberzegtl. The provision was repealed that a person representing a collective contract organisation should not become president of the main association of the Austrian social security funds. It is interesting, however, that in this context the parity, which was also the subject of this examination procedure, has not been criticised, so that a parity between employers and employees can be considered as quite constitutional in terms of six to six. ==References====External links== The third application, which was sent to the Constitutional Court on August 2003, was also contested. In the opinion of the Federal Government to the Constitutional Court, the Federal Government has, among other things, carried out the following – I quote –: “1. It is therefore not necessary, therefore, to organise the composition of the self-administrative bodies solely according to the ratio of the number of employees and service providers to each other. In the area of social security, a different picture appears, depending on the institution, with the legislator seeking a balanced consideration of the respective interests in the individual administrative bodies. 2. In the institutions of the social security institutions and the main association, the legislator has defined separately the proportion of employees and service providers to each other for each of these self-administrative bodies organised by these social partners. For example, the General Assembly and the Boards at the Pension Insurance Institute are composed of two-thirds of representatives of the servants and one-third of representatives of the service providers, whereas in the case of the territorial healthcare telephone, the said administrative bodies are made up of only one-fifths of representatives of the service providers. In this respect, parity exists at the General Insurance Institute. In addition, for this institution, the principle was explicitly established that the supervisor must belong to the group of service providers, taking into account the fact that the funds of accident insurance are paid exclusively from contributions from the service providers.’ In this context, it is also striking that the Obmann representative has not yet been re-occupied by the employees' representatives in this social security body, because workers' representatives have not been able to agree on one person. ‘Since the main association, as the umbrella association of social security institutions, has to take over the interests of the entire social security scheme across the board with functions of a holding company, the respective insurance representatives from the group of employees and service providers should also be represented as evenly as possible. Therefore, according to the Federal Government, parity within the meaning of a numerical equality between service representatives and service representatives both at the Annual General Meeting and in the Board of Directors is to be expected.’ So far as the opinion of the Federal Government to the Constitutional Court was concerned. It has now come to pass that the Social Democratic Party is on 14 May. On 1 November 2003, the Court of First Instance withdrew its application to the Constitutional Court, which wanted to review the parity. Moreover, following the recognition of the Constitutional Court and after the Constitutional Court has not abolished the parity in the administrative body. I therefore regard the parity as a constitutional form, at least in this form, because the bearer conference, like the general meeting of the social security funds of Austria, which was not withdrawn by the Constitutional Court, is re-established in the same composition, with the difference that, by combining the Railway Social Insurance Institute and the Mining Court, there are only two representatives of the former four representatives and three representatives of the seniors who were not yet entitled to vote. I can also quote here on the subject of the constitutional conformity of Professor Öhlinger from a contribution to the constitutional question of the reorganisation of the main association from 2001, that is, before the recognition of the Constitutional Court. Professor Öhlinger is more cautious about the parity of employees and service providers. Among other things, he executes: “For a parity of service providers and employees, social security also involves a balance between the interests of employers and employees.” Finally, he comes to the following conclusion: Whether in view of the enormous number of persons Overweight of the self-employed insured persons is still consistent with a parity of representatives of the servants and service providers in the administrative bodies of the main association, only the Constitutional Court of Justice could give binding answers. As explained above, the Constitutional Court has answered this question: He did not object to the parity and existence of the General Assembly. I therefore believe that this correction as a result of the Constitutional Court's knowledge, which has been given to Parliament in good time, is well-constitutional and is not a reshaping, but rather a reasonable balance of interest between different parities, as already stated, from 5: 1 or 3: 1 for the benefit of employees' representatives and in AUVA 1: 1 between employers' representatives and employees' representatives. I therefore believe that the criticism towards recoloration is wrong. One thing I note, however, is that the new main association will also include virilists who will represent the third force each resulting from the workers' and employers' elections, so that it will no longer be possible in the future for the main association of Austrian social security funds to be exclusively and alone The two largest groups in the employers' and employees' side are concerned. However, Mr Gusenbauer, I give you the right that it will no longer be possible for employees' representatives to perform the entire employee's representation by 60 or 70 percent of the votes, just as it will no longer be possible on the employer's side to exercise 100% of the power by 60 or 70 percent of the votes in the corresponding elections of the representative bodies. This is a reflection of the democratic elections, and this is a reflection of the balance of interests that self-government has. I therefore believe that we should not mix self-administration with the Austrian Parliament, but rather view self-government as what it is, as self-administration, which has never been specified in the Austrian Constitution since 1929 in its rights, its obligations and its possibilities to make up its composition, as just explained, but that there is a comprehensive and comprehensive Judicatur that causes me to consider the present draft as constitutional conformation to the main association of the Austrian social security institutions. I come to my conclusion, ladies and gentlemen. I believe that the structure presented here takes account of the needs of modern social security management. It's a good one, it's an acceptable reform.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e7507	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! The main reform of the association, as it stands today, has taken into account all the points raised by the Constitutional Court: The Carrier Conference reflects the Austrian social security landscape and also contains senior representatives. The Board of Directors has also taken into account the connection requested by the Constitutional Court of Justice of its members to social security schemes by either coming from the Board of Directors of the Controlling Group or the General Assembly of these insurance companies. In addition, the board of association has general competence, represents the main association outside and thus also has a right of guidance on the management of the association, which is bound to the instructions of the Board of Directors. The parity – my colleague Herbert Haupt has already done this – has not been questioned by the Constitutional Court, and I would like to expressly note that this parity is absolutely justified on the basis of the funds flowing into the social security system in Austria, because half of the employers pay in these social security schemes and half the employees. The main association is also the most important coordination body within social security funds. He has important tasks, such as the IT coordination within the social security funds. There has been a lot of success in the past: In the last two, three years, we have been able to bring together the many different IT systems, including in an ITSV, and in the end all Austrians will have the health card that e-Card will have by November 2005 at the latest. The trial operation will begin in the next two months, i.e. January and February. At the end of February, the roll-out begins in Burgenland and in further sequence, according to a precise plan, all over Austria. However, the main association will also have an important coordination function in the implementation of the Health Reform Act, which is still on the agenda today, where the Federal Health Agency and the National Health Platforms will be set up. – Mr Oellinger and Mrs Mandak: This has nothing to do with ‘ overcoloring ’! Achleitner.]] Now on the question that has been raised over and over again: Who decides the budget for the universities? – I can easily answer this question: Parliament in the University Law 2002. The universities are one of the few institutions that have a statutory budget that does not need to be re-adopted from budget year to budget year. In other words, in the University Act, Parliament decides for three years the basic budget for universities and what is still to come. I think that is a good thing because every three years this total budget for universities is discussed and decided on a broad basis. To the university budget, I can only say so much to you: The basic budget of €1.7 billion is still €63.7 million in additional allocations. There are 90 percent of the FWF's funds. All the third-party funds are added. The study contributions are included. At the end of the year, when universities present an accountable report for the first time, we will for the first time present an account of their budget, see what the universities really had on the budget. In the next few years, with €50 million for the infrastructure, additional money is available for the universities for which equipment is available. We have spent at least €200 million on buildings since 2000, and we will continue to invest a lot in the universities for the buildings. So I think that the university budget is a good budget and that we are setting new priorities at the universities. Ladies and gentlemen! ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e14614	Mr President. Ministers! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! On the ÖH Act, on information exchange and dialogue: On the part of the chairmen of the university's representatives, there have been suggestions that have been incorporated into the ÖH Act, material suggestions that have been taken very seriously. They were submitted on the basis of an amendment. And that is what I call democratic and constructive, which is in the sense of strengthening student representations at their universities. I would like to make a few points: The Presidency Conference of the University Offices remains as an important advisory body of the ÖH, and the Statute Authority exists. In other words, the university's representatives have the opportunity to adapt to the respective university or study offer structure in the best way they can perform their activities. This autonomy is now possible, because the approval of the ministry is no longer necessary. There will be a gradual increase in the funding for university representations from around 70 to 85 percent of ÖH contributions by 2007/2008. And at the suggestion of some student representatives, representations receive at least 30 percent of each ÖH's contribution. In addition, the base amount – regardless of the size of the university – will also be increased from 25 to 30 percent, which in particular will benefit smaller universities. A study representative should be set up for each degree, including teaching and doctoral studies. The representations may, by a two-thirds majority, sum up several studies in a representation. There are some more proposals that have been incorporated into the law: The e-mail addresses are provided by the rectorates to the study representatives, the establishment of clubs, the extension of the number of passive electors and so on. Ladies and gentlemen! The present bill reflects the new structure of universities and strengthens the direct representations. The money remains the same, the distribution is different. New priorities are possible. The individual student wins because there will be more service in his studies and more information about the University's contribution to the university's work because the university will ensure participation. Small unis and small groups benefit. There is no reference to the refusal of dialogue, nor to the reduction of democracy. An initiative proposal is a democratically legitimate instrument. Whoever will ultimately decide on the composition of the student representation, these are voters at the universities who, in the first place – and also I have had many conversations with students – wish a strong study representative who knows their concerns and problems on the ground and can directly implement them because they have the power and the means to do so. Ladies and gentlemen! Too many students have not felt represented for too long by their representation, for which they pay compulsory contributions. It is time for this representation to represent them again. The present ÖH law incorporates the ideas of the students. I am convinced that the impact of the law will be reflected in stronger student representations, increased participation in the ÖH elections and measures taken by individual universities for their students in not too much future. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e18869	Madam President. High house! Let me briefly comment on the comprehensive health package, which is here today for discussion and for the vote. This package includes a comprehensive health reform and I believe that this will make a lasting change in health care in Austria. Contrary to all the disadvantages that this health reform will no longer take place, because the government will no longer have power after the harmonisation of pensions, I am pleased to note today that this health reform is taking place, however, that it was possible to reach agreement with the countries on significant changes in the structures of health policy in our country and, at the same time, to send a number of laws on which health policy will determine in the coming years. The Article 15a Agreement, which could be concluded between the nine federal states and the Federal Government in the context of the financial compensation, includes a number of measures. On the one hand, and in my opinion, this is the most important thing – for the first time in the history of the Second Republic, there is joint planning, management and coordination of health care, both intra- and extra-mural, both the hospitals and the established doctors, and above all a cooperation in seam management. This not only means that we will provide a better service for patients, but also that it will ultimately be cost-saving, because performance shifts at the expense of the other partner will hopefully be history. However, this health package, the Article-15a agreement, also includes a quality assurance law or the agreement between the federal government and countries on a quality assurance law and on a health telematics law, two laws which my predecessors had never been able to enforce for years, because concerns about the costs incurred in the countries and the consultation mechanism were constantly raised. These negotiations have succeeded in moving countries to refrain from the consultation mechanism and thus to launch these two guiding laws. In addition to the financial package, which we decided in the House yesterday, a cost-reduction package is also provided, which includes in particular €300 million cost-reduction in the intramural area, by re-pointing the daily clinical services as a result of the change, the evaluation of the LKF point system, whereby at least one euro has to be saved for every euro that flows into the system in addition to money. Finally, there are also essential precautions such as a comprehensive mammography screening program for all women, a metabolic screening program for infants. All these are important measures which are important both for the federal and for the countries. The whole package, of course, also includes the Health Reform Act, which includes the implementation of the measures provided for in the Article-15a agreement. I am very pleased and I thank the opposition for being in line with Article 15a. However, it is very unfortunate that this is not the case with the Health Reform Act. In addition, the package also contains a PRIKRAF law, which incorporates the resulting prikraF regulation in the legislative text and thus ensures that the private health care institutions continue to make an important contribution in the future in the Austrian healthcare system. I am also very pleased that Mr Groenewald's request has succeeded in making a joint request to ensure the further expansion of the hospice industry and the comprehensive implementation. I think we're on the right track with that. This large health package, which includes important precautions with the next item on the agenda, namely the revision of the Tobacco Act, is in addition to the measures already put in place, namely the new precautionary examination, which is being evaluated and re-established, and together with the health passports from 1. January 2005 is available to all Austrians from 19 years of age and will also be associated with appropriate acceptance measures, a call and appeal system and thus hopefully also with a frequency increase – an important milestone in Austrian health policy. In addition, we will also set an important milestone in electronic development in the field of innovations presented with the health card, the next week in the model organisation, and will then go into the test operation by the end of February, and will ultimately implement a control group for the development of the electronic health acts. This is very important, because it will ultimately bring more safety to patients, who will also open up innovative opportunities, for example, that a settled doctor in a remote mountain valley can obtain a second opinion from a university clinic, that not every time he reaches the hospital will have to tell his health story again, that this will also avoid double findings, double conductivity, which will ultimately also lead to cost reductions. All of this, ladies and gentlemen, is a very, very important part of this major health reform package, according to the drug package that is taking place in the meantime. The health package stands, and I am very grateful to all those who have contributed. I would like to thank all those who have already contributed their ideas to the health conferences and dialogues, all who have participated in them. I would like to thank the political parties, the health speakers of their own group and the coalition partner. Karl Schweitzer, Herbert Haupt and Ursula Haubnerheb advise intensively with us. I would like to thank you very much for the cooperation. I also thank the opposition for the valuable inputs. I would also like to thank the countries, especially the Councils of State, who participated here, including the national officials. Above all, however, I would like to thank the officials of my home and the employees of my cabinet for the intensive work of the last weeks and months. We worked very hard on it for 16 months, sometimes until 3:00 a.m. It would be difficult for officials to put it under control, but this is where incredible things have been done. The national officials are also: sat on Sunday afternoon to complete Article 15a. No one has trusted us that we will eventually do so in ten days. Therefore: A great thank you to all those who contribute to this health package for the benefit of all Austrians with 1. ==References====External links==

ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e21509	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear women Federal ministers! Accounting Change Act – this is an EU implementation. I think she'll get a lot of approval. We need to adapt several EU targets here, namely, the IAS Regulation – that is the directive that regulates the accounting of consolidated accounts – then the Modernisation Directive – it lays down the balance sheet directives and the threshold value directive. It is true that the IAS Regulation applies directly to and for itself in Austria, but it is necessary that we adapt our Accounting Act where conflicting standards are. The Modernisation Directive concerns the annual accounts and consolidated accounts of companies with a special legal form, in particular banks and insurance undertakings. It is necessary that there is a special comparability that ensures transparency. These include, for example, provisions on annual report, progress report, confirmation note. The threshold value directive assigns the sizes of the undertakings to the individual provisions. This is what size capital companies have; then the rules are applied. There is no implementation obligation for us. We have made this voluntary, in order not only to ensure international standards, but also to ensure transparency. The government template and also the decision in the Committee have provided for us to make an adjustment in this regard. Finally, this law also serves certain adjustments in accordance with the Extraordinary Law, especially in the bankruptcy order and in the company book. I hope that all political groups will agree, and believe that we will give the economy a new, modern law.

Point Report of the Justice Committee on the Government Template (678, e.g._Other Organiser Federal Law amending the Reproduction Medicines Act (Prevention Medicine Act Novelle 2004 – FMedGNov 2004) (741 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e21814	We shall now proceed to item 28 of the agenda. There was no oral reporting. The first speaker was Mr Doppler. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-12-10-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00090_d7e21838	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) High house! Today, reproductive medicine already allows many couples, who are unwantedly childless for various reasons, to fulfill their desire for their own child. The legal basis for this is the Breeding Medicine Act of 1992. However, the law does not take into account those cases where there is no such current desire for children, but in view of illness and related therapy, it is foreseeable that the person concerned will be able to continue in the future. plantation by natural means will no longer be possible. This is the case, for example, with certain cancers. – With the new Reproductive Medicine Act, we can help. The constitutional bases for the artificial reproduction and restrictions of 1992 are still valid today, because it was already foreseeable that there would be an incessant development in this area. Essential content of the innovation: If we have so far had a storage period of one year, which has proved to be too short under certain conditions, this period will be extended. Since there are no ethical concerns to keep sperm cells and unfertilized ova for longer, it will leave to the patient how long the storage is desired. The storage of developing cells is limited to a period of ten years. This results in an improvement for carcinoma patients, but also for other patients, who are to be relieved later on, as well as for people with severe infectious diseases, who are inadequate in bringing about a pregnancy by natural means. With this change of law, we create benefits for a large group of people in fulfilling a child's desire. I ask the House to agree to this amendment of the reproductive medicine law. Thank you very much.

The ASFINAG is also a society that currently has debt, which is going to increase by €8.5 billion and is expected to increase to €12-13 billion by 2012. I do not believe that it is very intelligent if a society that is still financing itself, which still has to expect itself – by the year 2042, that is to say, “astronomic” periods of funding flows – involving further debt abroad – are involved, because this is only about further debts for which taxpayers have to guarantee again. Even the Federal Chamber of Commerce is very skeptical. – I have looked at their opinion, which reads: “As the debt burden so far has to continue to be borne and thus eradicated, it should be ensured that all ASFINAG companies contribute to this. Otherwise, there is a danger that in the foreseeable future the profitable activities will be transferred to foreign subsidiaries and Austrian toll revenue will be used primarily for the eradication of debts rather than for the expansion of the local infrastructure.” – Signed “Mitterlehner”. I can only fully support this. – This is reality. As long as Director-General Hecke speaks for cars at the ASFINAG of Road-Pricing, I would warn at all that he has any adventure abroad, because in no way can there be any risk business on the funds of Austrian drivers and possibly on road pricing for cars abroad. I think we should be careful. I'm not saying it should be that way forever, but it's currently, I think, too early to make such considerations. We will therefore not agree to this bill.
